Resolution tracking

In-scope markets that have settled, with the outcome recorded by Kalshi. The dashboard keeps tracking a market until its result field is populated, then reports it here permanently. Result is copied verbatim — it is never inferred from the last price.

Demo environment data — not real markets

Every price, volume and resolution below comes from Kalshi's demo exchange (external-api.demo.kalshi.co). Kalshi states that demo prices and behaviour “may not be reflective of those in real markets”. See demo environment docs. No real money is involved anywhere on this site.

Outcomes by topic

TopicYESNOotherTotalYES rate
Crypto539504001%
Gas prices81319040020%
Golf60340040015%
Elections84316040021%
Culture01010%

A high or low YES rate is a property of the contracts that happen to have settled in this demo, not a pattern. Sample sizes are shown so you can judge them; see Analysis for what these numbers can and cannot support.

How a resolution is recorded

resultEnum yes / no / scalar / empty. Source: Get Markets
Payout$1 per winning contract; losing side receives nothing. Source: Market settlement
RetentionSettled markets can move to /historical/markets after the published cutoff, so rows are archived as they are observed. Source: Historical data

All resolved in-scope markets (1601)

Read the volume column

1,499 of these 1,601 markets never traded. Their “final price” is the API's default 0.0000, not a price anyone paid — so the outcome alone is informative and the price is not. The Analysis page excludes them from calibration for that reason (F-15).

Sorted by last traded price. “Final price” is the last traded YES price reported for the settled market, not the payout.
